# Clocklings code review prompts 601–700

This standalone Markdown file is part 7 of 8 and contains 100 English review prompts. Start at 601 and process the supplied prompts through 700. If only one block is supplied, process that block. Use only the numbered scope supplied for this run.

Use the current working tree at C:\Dev\Clocklings. Read the current repository instructions and PROJECT.md. Before the first edit, inspect the branch and run git status --short. Capture the starting hash of PROJECT.md and a private, content-aware baseline for existing staged, unstaged and relevant untracked work. Build the path and evidence inventory this part needs from the current repository. Use the current part's ledger as the record of the checks performed in this run. Results from another part are optional evidence when their scope and revision can be verified.

Work through the numbered headings in order. Read the matching fenced block for each prompt and keep a Finnish ledger by prompt number. Record the paths inspected, evidence, confirmed findings, minimal changes, validation commands and actual outcomes. Record zero-change and blocked results as well. Give an uncovered contract relevant to this part an S-prefixed supplemental ID. Read the file in small ranges if needed. If interrupted, report the last fully completed ID, the unfinished or next ID, the existing changes and the validation completed. Resume from that boundary when asked to continue.

After completing 700, reconcile this part's ledger, inspect review-owned changes against the starting baseline and report the verification performed for this part. Work on another part begins when it is supplied.

## Contents

- [601–616 End-to-end journeys and test evidence](#end-to-end-journeys-and-test-evidence)
- [617–646 Screen and shared resource contracts](#screen-and-shared-resource-contracts)
- [647–693 Implementation and integration boundaries](#implementation-and-integration-boundaries)
- [694–700 Repository coverage and final reconciliation](#repository-coverage-and-final-reconciliation)

## End-to-end journeys and test evidence

### Cross-feature journeys and lifetime boundaries

#### 601 Can loss of an offline voice during a lesson leave playback controls unusable?

```text
Prompt 601 Can loss of an offline voice during a lesson leave playback controls unusable?

First re-read the repository-root PROJECT.md for this review item. Use it to understand product purpose, intended behavior, architecture, constraints, supported platforms and deliberate decisions. Treat it as a dated reference. Verify implementation against the current working tree, configuration, dependencies, tests and applicable owner instructions. Locate the actual equivalents of any renamed examples. If a documented facility is absent, establish that from the repository and report its applicability. Resolve discrepancies using current evidence. If the document appears stale or the requirement is unclear, preserve working behavior and record the uncertainty. Leave PROJECT.md untouched throughout this review unless the owner explicitly requests its modification in a separate task.

Use a local speech provider that becomes unavailable while the lesson remains open. Compare Replay, Listen, text and progression without changing the lesson's authored answer.

Follow the public user flow across feature, storage or service ownership boundaries. Check both the final user-visible outcome and the durable state in the same controlled scenario.

AI-assisted code review can hallucinate issues, misread context, or recommend unnecessary changes. Base every finding on the actual current repository and verify the full relevant code path, configuration, tests, version constraints, and existing safeguards before reporting or changing anything. It is completely acceptable to find no issue and make no change. Do not invent findings, force a quota, treat a preference as a defect, or modify code merely to produce visible work. Do not over-engineer, add speculative abstractions, or refactor code that is already correct, safe, clear, and fit for purpose. Prefer the smallest adequate solution. Less is better.

Follow reachable callers, inputs, state ownership, readers and writers through to the observable result. Check actual SDK/library versions, build variants, guards, validations, error paths, lifecycle and existing tests. Explain how a failure occurs or which established requirement is violated. Classify confirmed defects, unverified risks, style preferences and alternatives separately. Assign severity from concrete impact. Performance, leak, concurrency and security claims require a specific mechanism. If evidence is insufficient, record the gap and leave the code alone.

Inspect first. If and only if a real issue is confirmed, implement the smallest safe correction necessary to address its root cause. If no issue is confirmed, make no change. Do not perform opportunistic cleanup, unrelated refactoring, dependency updates, broad rewrites, or speculative improvements.

Preserve intended behavior, public interfaces, data formats, storage schema, UI and compatibility except where the confirmed defect requires a change. Fix a shared root cause once at its actual owner. Recheck a case already addressed by an earlier edit and mark it resolved only after verification. Avoid renames, file moves, helpers, layers and frameworks for their own sake. Architectural, dependency, migration, build, schema or public API changes require direct technical necessity and appropriate authorization. Review every changed file individually. Do not run broad automatic fixes.

Work only in C:\Dev\Clocklings. Before editing, inspect the branch and run git status --short. Preserve existing staged, unstaged and untracked user work. Before the first review edit, preserve a content-aware baseline of pre-existing work and the starting hash of PROJECT.md. Reuse it throughout the run to distinguish review edits from prior edits. Never discard, conceal, overwrite, stage or tidy unrelated work. Do not use git reset, restore, checkout, clean or stash to handle user changes. Do not commit, switch branches or alter history without a separate explicit request. No pushes or pull requests. On a branch that project rules protect from edits, continue read-only and record the edit blocker.

Keep the review local. Never deploy, publish, create accounts, change cloud services or production data, rotate keys or perform other external or irreversible actions. Leave steps requiring credentials, secrets, external services, an owner decision or separate safety confirmation pending. Record the exact dependency and continue safe local work. Use synthetic data and local fakes where appropriate. Do not access the archived application, signing keys or unrelated devices.

After each fix, run the smallest meaningful existing check through the affected entry point. Add or change a focused test only when needed to prove the fix or prevent recurrence. Run Gradle from this repository with --no-daemon. For a UI change, verify 320 dp at normal text, 200% text at a representative width, and 320 dp with 200% text together. Use only an explicitly identified dedicated Clocklings emulator for device work. Restore owned settings and stop any temporary processes or capture sessions you start. Reserve broader checks for the end of the run. Leave owner-run lc and sc to the owner. Report exact commands, outcomes and any environmental block. Separate source inspection, runtime proof, and pre-existing or unrelated failures. Leave unrelated failures alone.

Record the result in Finnish in the run ledger under this prompt number. Name the area and inspected files or paths. State whether an issue was confirmed. If none was confirmed, state that no change was made and whether the inspected implementation met its contract. For each finding give its evidence, reachable trigger, exact path and symbol or line, root cause, impact and realistic severity. List every changed file with its reason, the correction and actual validation result. Include commands run, checks not run and why, pre-existing or unrelated failures, and anything unverified.

After inspection, any necessary minimal correction, validation and this entry, state that you are continuing and automatically process the next numbered prompt if supplied. Do not wait for routine approval. If the run cannot continue, stop at an explicit numbered boundary and report the last fully completed ID, the unfinished or next outstanding ID, edits and checks actually run. Never skip an item or claim completion for unprocessed work.
```
